## Added arrows and the constant loss of a coordinate cutoff

```mermaid
flowchart TD
 AF[Restriction-closed: B <= lambda H + K I] -->|C=(K+2/ln2)/(1-lambda)| FEI[FEI C]
 WL[Weak regime: exists i, B_i <= lambda H/I + K times alpha_i] -->|C=max log2(e/tau)+2/ln2, (K+2/ln2)/(1-lambda)| FEI
 WLG[Same weak rule; i must lie in G_c] -->|same forward C| FEI
 FEI -->|lambda=0; K=C/(1-c)| WLG
 BF[Weak regime: B_G <= lambda H + K I; lambda<1-c] -->|C=max log2(e/tau)+2/ln2, (K+(2/ln2)(1-c))/(1-c-lambda)| FEI
 P4[OPEN: c=1/2, tau=1/4, K=4, lambda=0] -->|C=4+2/ln2| FEI
 MAX[Fixed-price maximum-influence coordinate] --> BAD[REFUTED by majority-diluted B05]
```

All lambda in the first three arrows lie in [0,1); K>=0; tau in (0,1). In the restricted-bucket arrow lambda in [0,1-c). All quantified source classes must contain every reached restriction needed by the induction, or be all Boolean functions. A root-only property is not a class theorem.

Proof strategy: if H>CI, choose a violating function with least relevant dimension and write delta=H-CI>0. Each actual child satisfies the C bound, giving Delta_i>=C alpha_i+delta and B_i>= (C-a)alpha_i+delta for every relevant i. Summing contradicts B<=lambda H+KI at C=(K+a)/(1-lambda). For a weak coordinate satisfying the local fraction inequality, Delta_i<=(lambda C+K+a)alpha_i+lambda delta alpha_i/I; the displayed C makes it strictly smaller than C alpha_i+delta. Outside the weak regime a maximum influence at least tau has B_i<=alpha_i log2(e/tau), which supplies the strong-coordinate constant. This proves the lead's arrows without a circular assumed bound on H/I at the current node.

The high-bucket fraction beta=I_G/I is at least 1-c+c/d>1-c. Weighted averaging gives a cutoff coordinate at price B_G/(beta I). Therefore FEI(C) already implies high-cutoff selection at K=C/(1-c). Adding that cutoff with an unspecified finite K is FEI-equivalent in finiteness, not a genuinely stronger finiteness theorem. This is an explicit qualification of the lead's proposed example. At a prescribed budget it is a stronger operational requirement; P4 freezes that budget rather than hiding an unknown FEI constant. No canonical smallest lemma exists over all conceivable formulations. The exact first stronger claim in this frozen route is P4 itself.

For P4, the strong branch costs 2+3/ln2, below 4+2/ln2 because ln2>1/2, and the weak branch costs 4+2/ln2. Hence its forward universal constant is exactly the stated sufficient value. The diagram does not supply P4 from FEI at that value with the same K=4.

## Tests consume known minima and add eligibility

With delta=2^-k,p=delta^2,s=sqrt(delta), u=1/2+delta,A=1-s+delta, canonical B05 has alpha_data=pu, alpha_control=pA, alpha_selector=pdelta and I=p[k u+2kA+delta] on 3k+1 relevant coordinates. Data eligibility at c=1/2 is the strictly positive identity

    2(3k+1)u-[k u+2kA+delta]
      =k(1/2+2s+3delta)+1+delta>0.

Controls are larger and eligible. The already-computed erasure minimizer (data for k=4, control for even k>=6) is therefore always eligible and has price below 2+7/1024<4. All root influences are below 1/4. This passes P4 at those roots, not at unexamined descendants. X009's data coordinates are eligible with price below 2, but their influence exceeds 1/2: that root is outside the weak regime and is labelled accordingly.

## New retained-coordinate identity and maximum-rule obstruction

Replace background inputs of any Boolean f(y,x) by disjoint balanced Boolean encoder blocks and retain y. Every encoded spectral frequency determines its outer label by whether each block is nonempty. Its two y-sector coefficients share the same encoder multiplier. Binary edge entropy is homogeneous, so summing squared multipliers by Parseval proves B_y, alpha_y, Delta_y and JS_y are EXACTLY unchanged. Background influence multiplies by its encoder bit's influence. This is a genuine Boolean composition, not a real normalized mixture.

For odd Majority_M, every nonzero spectral set is odd. Adjacent internal frequencies can both be occupied only when they are empty and singleton. With rho=binom(2R,R)/2^(2R), M=2R+1, each inner influence and singleton coefficient equals rho. Therefore

    B_inner=sum_(T not containing j) Phi(p_T,rho^2p_(T+j)),
    alpha_inner=rho alpha_j,
    B_inner/alpha_inner
      <=rho[log2(1/alpha_j)+2log2(1/rho)+2/ln2].

The bound follows from Phi(u,tv)<=tv/ln2+tv[log2(1/t)+log2(1+u/v)], then L_j<=B_j<=h2(alpha_j). All other outer coordinates, including y, stay in the sum.

Now retain B05's selector and replace all 3k backgrounds with Majority_M for R=2^(2k+2), M=2^(2k+3)+1. Induction from the central-binomial recurrence proves rho<=1/sqrt(R+1)<delta/2; also M rho>=1. Original background influences lie in [p/2,p]. Thus the selector has unchanged alpha=pdelta, every other alpha is strictly less than pdelta/2, and the selector is the unique maximum-influence input. Its ratio remains at least (1-delta)(k-2), diverging. All influences tend to zero; total I also tends to zero by the upper bound in DERIVATION section 4. This analytically refutes, for every fixed finite K and every tau>0, the proposal that a maximum-influence coordinate of every weak function can satisfy B_i<=K alpha_i. It also refutes the same fixed-price maximum rule for Delta_i. It does not refute a lambda H/I allowance or FEI.

On that SAME family every encoded background lies above I/(2D), D=3kM+1, since M rho k(5u-2A)+2u rho>=k/2>delta. The new inner bound gives every background ratio <=(4k+3+2/ln2)/2^(k+1)<1. Thus P4 has many eligible cheap coordinates while its unique largest input is arbitrarily expensive. This is a checked all-parameter separation, not a floating search pattern.

The first unsupported line is P4 for arbitrary weak Boolean functions and their reached restrictions. Balanced encoder blocks cease to be balanced after one inner input is physically restricted; their induced mean is +/-rho, so the root invariants above cannot be silently reused. No universal high-bucket fraction or full C018 payment is proved. Stage 1's C020 coding-debt inequality remains open and is not claimed advanced by this localization.

# A004 constant and unit ledger

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

This is the finite, reusable normalization audit assigned to A004/B01, not a new candidate proof of FEI. For every n>=0 and every Boolean sign f on the uniform cube, use a_S=E[f chi_S], P_S=a_S^2, H=sum P_S log2(1/P_S), I=sum |S|P_S, v=Var(f)=1-a_empty^2, and delta=I-v. Constants satisfy H=I=v=delta=0, so no ratio is formed there. All zero entropy summands are zero; conditional laws of mass zero are omitted. The universal target remains existence of one finite C with H<=CI in every dimension.

The two requested deliverables are CONVENTIONS.json and ENDPOINT_CHECKS.md. The report and code give their derivations and certificates. Scope: normalization of the current threshold/percolation interfaces, the source erasure and physical-block identities, the posterior forest residual, the prior-mixture interface, and the history endpoint. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Logarithm units

For every probability/subprobability law, H_nat=(ln2)H_bits, and similarly for binary entropy, KL, JS, mutual information, TC, DTC and entropy-functionals. An influence or variance has no logarithm and does not change. Thus H_nat<=c I converts to H_bits<=c/(ln2) I; a bit constant converts to a natural constant by multiplication by ln2. If both sides are information quantities the coefficient is unchanged. A mixed charge a TC_nat+b I becomes a TC_bits+(b/ln2)I after dividing the entire inequality by ln2. The scalar ln2 factors in rank discrepancies, complete codes and normalization charges must therefore be converted together.

Checked explicit bit constants:

| Interface | Exact bit constant | Certified upper bound |
|---|---|---|
| Spectral-level entropy | Clev=log2(4+e) | 2.749 |
| Regular/weighted uniform threshold | Creg=Clev+2log2(e*pi) | 8.937 |
| Symmetric product-biased thresholds | CT=20(23+ln16)/ln2=80+460/ln2 | 744 |
| Erasure recombination | 2/ln2 | 2.886 |
| AND/OR corrected entropy | 2+4/ln2 | 7.771 |
| Parity corrected entropy | 2/ln2 | 2.886 |
| Complete forest base cost | Kref=3+2log2(3)+log2(6e) | 10.198 |

The decimal bounds in this table are outward rational bounds, not nearest-rounded interval endpoints. out/validation.json stores exact rational enclosures for Clev, Creg, CT, Kref and the erasure constant. The other displayed bounds follow from the same ln2 interval; their proof can be checked directly. In particular CT is already a bit constant: dividing 744 by ln2 a second time would be incorrect.

### 2. Signs, indicators, raw entropy and normalized entropy

Let g=(f+1)/2 with p=Eg. Then ghat(empty)=p, fhat(empty)=2p-1 and fhat(S)=2ghat(S) for S nonempty. Parseval gives sum ghat(S)^2=p, whereas sum fhat(S)^2=1. Consequently Var(f)=4p(1-p)=4Var(g), and the spectral Dirichlet energy I_spec(g)=sum |S|ghat(S)^2 is I(f)/4. Probability of a pivotal flip, however, is identical for f and g. The label "indicator influence" must therefore specify boundary probability or Dirichlet energy.

For a nonnegative vector q of total mass M>0, define Ent_raw(q)=sum q log2(1/q). Direct substitution gives

    H(q/M)=Ent_raw(q)/M+log2 M.

It is false to call Ent_raw(ghat^2) Shannon entropy without this normalization. For the nonempty sum Hplus(f), the exact scaling is

    Hplus(f)=4 Ent_raw(ghat(S)^2: S nonempty)-2 Var(f).

The additive term comes from log2(4)=2; pure factor-four scaling is false. Let H_g be Shannon entropy of the normalized full indicator spectrum ghat^2/p. Its empty atom has mass p, and its nonempty conditional law agrees with that of the sign. Hence

    H(f)=h2(v)+4p[H_g-h2(p)],   v=4p(1-p).

At p=0 the normalized indicator law is undefined and omitted. At p=1 its law is the empty atom and H_g=0. Both constant signs have H=0. The same nonempty affine scaling holds for corrected strong entropy with any fixed product basis and correction factor sigma_S^2; empty coefficients still require their own treatment.

### 3. Empty versus nonempty

Let R_S=P_S/v on S nonempty when v>0, and E0=(1-v)log2(1/(1-v)). Then

    H=h2(v)+vH(R),
    Hplus=vH(R)+v log2(1/v),
    H=Hplus+E0.

For v=0 the conditional law R is omitted; H=Hplus=E0=0. For v=1, E0=0 and H=Hplus=H(R). Thus the h2(v) term belongs to conditional nonempty entropy, whereas adding h2(v) to the raw nonempty sum double-counts v log2(1/v).

The universal bound E0<=v/ln2 follows from -(1-v)ln(1-v)<=v. Poincare v<=I gives E0<=I/ln2. Therefore Hplus<=K I implies H<= (K+1/ln2)I. More usefully Hplus<=K(I-v) implies

    H<=max(K,1/ln2) I.

The source also gives E0<=beta(I-v), beta=sqrt(2/(1-ln2))<2.553 (Lemma 7.6), so Hplus<=K(I-v) implies H<=(K+beta)(I-v). This explains the source's clean 744 and 747 constants.

For completeness, the empty-coefficient estimate uses entropy tensorization for the minority indicator: p ln(1/p)<= (ln2)I/2. Each pivotal fair fiber contributes (ln2)/2 to conditional entropy; this proves I>=2p log2(1/p). Put x=1-2p=|Ef| and a=1-ln2. Subtract v=1-x^2 to obtain

    I-v >= (1-x)[-ln(1-x)-x ln2]/ln2
          >= (1-x)(a x+x^2/2)/ln2.

The scalar inequality -sqrt(x) ln x<=1-x follows by x=exp(-2t), t<=sinh t. It gives E0/(I-v)<=2sqrt(x)/(a+x/2)<=sqrt(2/a) by AM-GM. The endpoints x=0,1 have E0=0. This proof treats parity and dictators, where I-v may vanish, without dividing by zero.

The other universal variance charge is h2(v)<=2I. The source proves the stronger I>=v+(v/2)log2(1/v): compare edge isoperimetry using x=sqrt(1-v) and the concave function (x-1)log2(1-x)+(1+x)log2(1+x)-2x, zero at x=0,1. Since h2(v)<=v log2(1/v)+v/ln2, the stronger comparison yields h2(v)<=2I+(1/ln2-2)v<=2I. This charge may be added once to a nonempty conditional code; it cannot be silently repeated at every witness choice.

### 4. Total, directional and biased influence

On the uniform cube, rho_i=P[f(X)!=f(X^i)]=E[((f_+-f_-)/2)^2]=sum_{S contains i}P_S. Total I=sum rho_i. If E_i counts undirected boundary edges in direction i, rho_i=E_i/2^(n-1). The sum over all vertices counts both orientations. The output-oriented probability P[f(X)=-1,f(X^i)=1] is rho_i/2. A monotone lower-side pivotal fiber fraction is instead rho_i. These are distinct common meanings of "directional". Independent fair resampling changes f with probability rho_i/2; its squared sign difference has expectation 2rho_i.

For independent Bernoulli inputs with parameter p_i, use phi_i=(X_i-p_i)/sqrt(p_i(1-p_i)). The pivotal event depends only on other coordinates. If its probability is rho_i, the squared Fourier inclusion mass is

    I_spec,i=4p_i(1-p_i)rho_i.

This follows by integrating i: its sign coefficient equals 2sqrt(p_i(1-p_i)) times the Fourier coefficient of (f_+-f_-)/2 on the other coordinates. Reversal change probability is rho_i, but independent resampling change probability is 2p_i(1-p_i)rho_i=I_spec,i/2. Biased output-oriented flip probabilities need not agree: for an increasing sign they are (1-p_i)rho_i and p_i rho_i. This explains why the uniform half-orientation rule cannot be imported under biases.

The threshold paper's I_mu is I_spec. The percolation paper's I is sum rho_i and its J is sum I_spec,i. They agree at p_i=1/2. On a uniformly interior bias interval [p0,1-p0],

    4p0(1-p0) I_piv <= I_spec <= I_piv.

The comparison constant depends on p0. Sending p0 to zero cannot retain it. Nor do equal one/two-point inclusion statistics imply equality of spectral and pivotal laws or their entropy. For a uniform dictator, the spectral set is deterministic but the output is a fair sign: H(S)=0 and H(f(X))=1.

### 5. Corrected strong entropy and character centering

Under product biases define sigma_i^2=4p_i(1-p_i) and

    Hcorr=sum_{S nonempty}P_S log2(product_{i in S}sigma_i^2/P_S)
         =Hplus+sum_i I_spec,i log2 sigma_i^2.

This is the threshold paper's Hplus_mu, not ordinary conditional entropy or ordinary Shannon entropy. E_mu=(ln2)Hcorr. For Boolean signs |fhat_mu(S)|<=E|phi_S|=product_i sigma_i, so each corrected summand is nonnegative. A biased dictator has P_empty=(2p-1)^2, P_{i}=4p(1-p), I_spec=v=4p(1-p), delta=0, Hcorr=0, and ordinary Shannon entropy h2(4p(1-p))>0 except at its special deterministic spectral endpoints. This refutes importing the biased corrected theorem as H_Shannon<=C delta. At p=0 or 1 fix the deterministic input and remove its basis coordinate before using these identities.

On the uniform cube character multiplication sends P_S to P_{S xor U}. It preserves full H but can change v, Hplus and I. Its new ith influence is 1-rho_i if i belongs to U, and rho_i otherwise. Therefore

    D=sum min(rho_i,1-rho_i)=min_U I(chi_U f).

One also has D<=4(I-v): |fhat({i})|<=rho_i, and min(rho_i,1-rho_i)<=2rho_i(1-rho_i)<=2(rho_i-fhat({i})^2). Summing gives at most twice the degree-at-least-two size-weighted mass; |S|<=2(|S|-1) there gives the bound. A dictator/character can be centered to a constant; its I changes while H remains zero. Character translation of the biased basis does not give this uniform XOR rule.

### 6. Universal bridge constants and first unsupported lines

For C018 use the source notation B_i=H(S_i|S_without_i), full spectral law including the empty atom. With u=fhat(T), w=fhat(T+i), m=u^2+w^2, the two child laws have atoms (u+w)^2,(u-w)^2. Chain rule gives Delta_i=B_i+JS(children). The scalar inequality [(1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z)]/2<=1-sqrt(1-z^2), obtained by derivative comparison, yields

    0<=JS<= (2/ln2)sum_T min(P_T,P_{T+i})
          <= (2/ln2)min(rho_i,1-rho_i).

Thus a universal B<=K_bits I would imply full FEI with C_bits<=K_bits+2/ln2 by choosing a weighted-average good coordinate and inducting with I=E I(child)+rho_i. In natural units the same conclusion is C_nat<=K_nat+2. No new bound on K is proved. The first unsupported line is the universal B<=K I, not the unit conversion or induction. The scalar additive constant's sharpness is not a claimed optimal Boolean FEI constant.

For C019 a genuine physical stopping tree obeys I(root)=sum_internal w_v I_{A_v}(f_v)+E I(leaf). Therefore a per-node entropy decrement bound K_bits times consumed influence and a terminal bound C_leaf I(leaf) yield max(K_bits,C_leaf)I(root). For blocks the exact decrement is H(S_A|S_complement)+MI(physical restriction; remaining spectrum), and the source channel cost is at most (2/ln2)I_A. The conditional spectral term is unpaid; replacing it by erasures of child laws is invalid. If the tree charges are natural, convert their whole sum before combining with a bit terminal bound. The local bent-defect no-go from B05 remains intact after unit conversion; no constant rescaling repairs a divergent ratio.

For C020 the campaign's forest interface is H<=h2(v)+Kref I+R. Hence R_nat<=k_nat I would imply C_bits<=2+Kref+k_nat/ln2. Both v(Lambda+log2 Z) and the posterior entropy subtraction in R must be in the same base, with distinct actual finite runs as witnesses. The rank discrepancy F written with ln must retain gamma/ln2 in a bit cost. The negative normalizer log2 Z is not a missing positive charge and cannot be bounded separately after cancellation. Universal payment of the complete R is the first unsupported line.

The seed prior-mixture bound H<=h2(v)+sum_nonempty P_S inf_j[b_j(S)+log2(1/w_j)] uses a fixed subprobability reference for each j and a globally normalized prior sum w_j<=1. For a countable dyadic prior, scale penalties and all normalizers stay inside the selected complete cost. If the entire conditional cost expectation is <=K I, the full bound is (K+2)I; no independent selection of cheapest components is licensed.

For C024 the gamma/history notes use natural units for TC, DTC, KL losses and Gamma. The population size J_t=sum marginals is log-free. Dividing the complete generator/integral identity by ln2 converts its information terms; any coefficient multiplying J0 also divides by ln2. Its paid weighted Gamma dissipation budget is not the weighted Gamma lifetime in the desired signed remainder. The first unpaid line is payment of that signed lifetime minus forced DTC-information losses, followed by a separate transfer to spectral H. Changing units closes neither line.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Physical split and the three different charges

Let g=f|x_i=+1, h=f|x_i=-1, psi=(g+h)/2, phi=(g-h)/2. Then

    f=psi+x_i phi,
    psi,phi take values in {-1,0,1},
    psi phi=0 and psi^2+phi^2=1 pointwise,
    I_i=E phi^2, E psi^2=1-I_i.

The disjointness is on physical inputs, not on Fourier supports. With u_T=psihat(T), w_T=phihat(T), T excluding i,

    fhat(T)=u_T, fhat(T+i)=w_T,
    ghat(T)=u_T+w_T, hhat(T)=u_T-w_T.

Put a=u_T^2, b=w_T^2, m=a+b and Phi(a,b)=(a+b)h2(b/(a+b)), with zero-mass terms omitted. Define

    B_i=sum_T Phi(a,b)=H(S_i|S_without_i),
    L_down,i=sum_T b log2(1+a/b),
    C_up,i=sum_T a log2(1+b/a),
    B_i=L_down,i+C_up,i.

Zero numerator/denominator boundary values are zero for the corresponding weighted summand. Since ln(1+t)<=t, 0<=C_up,i<=I_i/ln2. Thus L_down,i<=B_i<=L_down,i+I_i/ln2. A003 and C018 Section 5 use this downward L. It is not B05's L_mismatch.

Indeed B05's child packet has q_T=(ghat(T)^2+hhat(T)^2)/2=m and e_T=(ghat(T)-hhat(T))^2/4=b. Therefore its L_mismatch,i=sum q_T h2(e_T/q_T) is exactly B_i. The two source meanings coincide only accidentally in degenerate cases. Already on AND2, B_i=L_mismatch,i=1 but L_down,i=1/2.

Let Q_plus,Q_minus be the normalized squared child spectra. Their average is m, and

    JS_i=H(m)-[H(Q_plus)+H(Q_minus)]/2,
    Delta_i=H(P)-[H(Q_plus)+H(Q_minus)]/2=B_i+JS_i.

The equal physical split weights 1/2 are essential. Here JS_i is information in bits and Delta_i is a full physical entropy decrement; neither is the spectral deficit I-Var. The source bound is 0<=JS_i<=(2/ln2)min(I_i,1-I_i). Stage 1 already reconstructed it; its proof is reused without claiming a new result.

In natural units all B,L_down,C_up,JS,Delta multiply by ln2. Consequently C_up,nat<=I_i and JS_nat<=2min(I_i,1-I_i). A universal downward price L_nat<=k I would give B_nat<=(k+1)I and FEI_nat<=(k+3)I. A universal mismatch/erasure price L_mismatch,nat<=k I instead gives FEI_nat<=(k+2)I. Equating the two L symbols loses one paid influence term in the proof ledger. These implications were already sourced in A003; the delta here is a machine-readable common convention with endpoint regressions.

### 2. The exact slack in B_i<=h2(I_i)

The spectral bit S_i is Bernoulli(I_i), so conditioning immediately gives

    B_i<=h2(I_i),
    h2(I_i)-B_i=MI(S_i;S_without_i)>=0.

An equivalent packet proof uses concavity of h2: sum m_T=1 and sum b_T=I_i, giving sum m_T h2(b_T/m_T)<=h2(I_i). Equality means the spectral bit is independent of the remaining spectrum. For an actual Boolean sign the source's exact dyadic rigidity then forces I_i in {0,1/2,1}. This is a reuse of a proved source endpoint, not a new approximation theorem. The source's bent-splice example already shows that arbitrarily small mutual-information slack does not imply a fixed local B_i/I_i price.

Summing this marginal bound is not a new FEI proof. For point defects, sum_i h2(I_i)/I grows like n, while B/I tends to 2. The joint conditional information must remain in the ledger.

### 3. DTC, TC, character influence and invariances

For the full normalized spectral law define

    B=sum_i B_i,
    DTC=H(P)-B=sum_i H(S_without_i)-(n-1)H(P),
    TC=sum_i h2(I_i)-H(P),
    sum_i MI(S_i;S_without_i)=TC+DTC.

For n=0 both information quantities are zero. DTC>=0 follows from the chain rule: for any order, DTC=sum_i MI(S_i;S_after_i | S_before_i). DTC=0 is equivalent to a product law; exact Boolean product rigidity is already closed by ERASURE_RIGIDITY Section 1 and the A002 correction. The B01 lead's exact-product OPEN label is stale, while its quantitative low-influence DTC target remains OPEN.

Use the distinct symbol D_char=sum_i min(I_i,1-I_i)=min_U I(chi_U f), a log-free influence quantity. Under uniform character translation P_S -> P_{S xor U}, H, B_i, DTC, TC, Delta_i, JS_i and D_char are invariant. If i belongs to U, L_down,i and C_up,i swap; otherwise they merely reindex and are individually invariant. Hence L_down is generally not invariant, even though B is. The physical psi/phi likewise swap at a queried bit in U, with a common remaining character factor. Conditioning on a nonempty spectral set is not character invariant because the removed atom changes from P_empty to P_U. These are full-law statements, not biased-basis translation claims.

### 4. Exact empty-atom correction for erasure and DTC

For any probability law P on subsets, let a=P_empty, v=1-a>0, R(S)=P(S)/v for S nonempty, R(empty)=0. Define the empty-row correction

    T0(P)=sum_i Phi(P_empty,P_{i}).

Split each erasure sum into its T=empty pair and the other pairs. Phi is homogeneous: Phi(vr,vs)=v Phi(r,s). The conditional R's empty pair is (0,R_{i}) and has zero erasure. Therefore, exactly,

    B_i(P)=v B_i(R)+Phi(P_empty,P_{i}),
    B(P)=v B(R)+T0(P),
    DTC(P)=h2(v)+v DTC(R)-T0(P).

The downward law separately obeys

    L_down,i(P)=v L_down,i(R)+P_{i} log2(1+P_empty/P_{i}).

Its complementary correction is P_empty log2(1+P_{i}/P_empty). Their sum is the erasure correction. At v=0 omit R and all full-law quantities are zero. This exact algebra holds for all finite set laws; R is not asserted to be a Boolean spectrum, so Boolean rigidity cannot be applied to R alone.

Actual Boolean counterexample to discarding the correction: AND2 has all four spectral atoms 1/4, v=3/4, H(P)=B(P)=2, DTC(P)=0. Its nonempty law is uniform on three subsets, H(R)=log2 3 and B(R)=4/3, so DTC(R)=log2 3-4/3>0. The sign is certified by 3^3=27>16=2^4. Here T0=1 and h2(v)+vDTC(R)=1, which cancel exactly. This is a normalization counterexample, not a refutation of the properly defined C018 or FEI.

### 5. Actual Boolean all-dimensional stress family

For the point defect f_n=1-2*1_{x=x0}, n>=2, put p=2^-n. P_empty=(1-2p)^2 and every nonempty atom is 4p^2. R_n is uniform on 2^n-1 nonempty subsets. Therefore

    B_i(R_n)=(1-2p)/(1-p),
    DTC(R_n)=log2(2^n-1)-n(1-2p)/(1-p)
            =n p/(1-p)+log2(1-p).

Let ell=log2(e). The convergent logarithm series gives explicit all-n bounds

    p(n-ell)+(n-ell/2)p^2/(1-p)
      <=DTC(R_n)<=p(n-ell)+n p^2/(1-p).

Indeed the difference from p(n-ell) is n p^2/(1-p)-ell sum_{k>=2}p^k/k, and 0<=sum<=p^2/[2(1-p)]. Thus DTC(R_n)~n p while H(R_n)~n.

For the full law write m0=(1-2p)^2+4p^2 and b0=Phi((1-2p)^2,4p^2). Exact erasure and downward formulas are

    B_i(P_n)=b0+4p(1-2p),
    L_down,i(P_n)=4p^2 log2(m0/(4p^2))+2p(1-2p),
    T0(P_n)=n b0,
    H(P_n)=E0+8p(1-p)(n-1), I(P_n)=2np.

The binary-entropy estimate gives 0<=b0<=4p^2(2n-2+ell), since m0<=1. Stage 1's exact E0 formula has E0=4p ell+O(p^2). Consequently H(P_n)~8np, B(P_n)~4np, DTC(P_n)~4np, vDTC(R_n)~4np^2 and T0=O(n^2p^2). In particular

    DTC(P_n)/H(P_n)->1/2,
    vDTC(R_n)/H(P_n)->0,
    DTC(P_n)/(vDTC(R_n))~2^n.

An unweighted near-product claim for the nonempty law is thus not a replacement for the full-law DTC endpoint, even on genuine Boolean signs with I->0. Conversely AND2 shows conditional DTC positive while full DTC vanishes. This settles the normalization question in both directions; it does not bound the unpaid nonempty adjacent spectral pairs by influence.

### 6. First unsupported line

The complete erasure-fraction target still requires constants c>0,K,tau with full-law DTC(P_f)>=cH(P_f)-KI(f) on the source-specified low-influence/minimal-violator regime. The identities above do not prove this. Substituting DTC(R_f) without h2(v)-T0 is invalid. The equivalent C018 edge obligation at nonempty lower endpoint |S|>=1 remains unpaid; the B20 root correction is already a paid component and is not claimed as new progress here. The C019 stopping route still requires a single-use payment at actual descendants plus full terminal H. Maj3's B=0,H=2 is an immediate terminal regression.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

A005, B01, noncircular endpoint referee. Followed the B01 lead's v1 2026-10-11 22:40Z direction: referee low-influence localization. All functions below are finite Boolean signs on uniform independent cubes. Entropy is in bits. Put p(S)=fhat(S)^2, d_i=Inf_i, I=sum d_i, B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), and a=2/ln(2). Constants have H=I=0 and are handled separately.

For 0<tau<1, 0<=lambda<1, K>=0, define L(tau,lambda,K): every nonconstant f with max_i d_i<tau has a relevant coordinate i such that

    B_i(f)/d_i <= lambda H(f)/I(f) + K.

The global version removes the low-influence hypothesis. The questions are (i) whether the low-influence class admits arbitrary seeds with H/I preserved, (ii) whether admission transports this selectable-coordinate criterion with the same lambda,K, and (iii) what constant follows rigorously from localization. This is a source-complete referee artifact, not a full-FEI proof or a new broad conjectural endpoint. The global same-parameter implication remains unproved here.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## 1. Localization implies FEI, with explicit constants

For every i, B_i<=h2(d_i): conditional entropy is at most marginal entropy. The function h2(d)/d decreases on (0,1), because its derivative is log2(1-d)/d^2<0. Consequently a coordinate with d_i>=tau has B_i/d_i<=c_tau:=h2(tau)/tau. The source's restriction identity is

    Delta_i H = B_i + JS_i, 0<=JS_i<=a d_i,
    I = d_i + (I(f_+)+I(f_-))/2.

Assuming L(tau,lambda,K), set

    C = max{ c_tau+a, (K+a)/(1-lambda) }.

Prove H<=CI by induction in dimension, applying induction to ALL children, whether or not they stay low-influence. If some d_i>=tau, choose that coordinate and use Delta_i<=C d_i. Otherwise choose the localized witness. With r=d_i/I in (0,1], induction gives

    H <= C(I-d_i) + (lambda H/I + K+a)d_i,
    H(1-lambda r) <= CI + (K+a-C)d_i
                          <= CI(1-lambda r).

Since 1-lambda r>0, H<=CI. This supplies a slightly sharper high-influence price than the lead's log2(e/tau)+a, without changing the mechanism. It avoids falsely treating {max d_i<tau} as restriction closed. Conversely FEI(C) implies L(tau,0,C): B=sum B_i<=H<=CI, and influence-weighted averaging gives min_i B_i/d_i<=C. Thus existence of finite localized parameters is equivalent to finiteness of FEI, with the displayed quantitative loss. No assumption that C_FEI is already finite is made.

## 2. Arbitrary-seed H/I admission retains the exact FEI constant

For any nonconstant seed f, put g_m=z product_{j=1}^m f(x^(j)), where z is a fresh fair sign, and activate it at one point of k controls: F=1+A_k(g_m-1), t=2^-k. Tensor independence and balancing give H(g_m)=mH(f), I(g_m)=1+mI(f), and g_m is balanced. Direct Fourier expansion gives

    H(F)=t mH(f)+2kt(2-t)+phi((1-t)^2),
    I(F)=t(mI(f)+1+k),
    d_data=t d_i(f), d_z=t, d_control=t,

where phi(x)=-x log2 x. Take k=ceil(2log2(m+1)). Then I(F)->0, max_i d_i(F)->0, minority probability=t/2->0, and H(F)/I(F)->H(f)/I(f), since k/m->0 and phi((1-t)^2)/t->2/ln2. Therefore the supremum H/I on max d_i<tau is exactly C_FEI as an extended-real supremum, for every tau>0. Any FEI inequality H<=CI on that class transfers with the SAME C. This is the established baseline applied to the specific endpoint, not a second claim of novelty.

That argument does NOT transfer L(tau,lambda,K) with the same lambda,K. Its conclusion is an H/I limit, whereas its premise controls one coordinate selected from an enlarged set. The extra coordinates must be audited.

## 3. Exact control erasure: cheap coordinates introduced by activation

For a balanced core g and k controls, put E(u,v)=phi(u^2)+phi(v^2)-phi(u^2+v^2). Each control has

    B_control(F)=2t-2t^2+E(1-t,t), d_control=t,
    B_control/d_control=2-2t+E(1-t,t)/t -> 2.

The nonempty-data spectrum contributes t: for each retained set the two control-membership amplitudes have equal magnitude. The empty-data pairs other than the exceptional constant pair contribute t-2t^2. The exceptional pair contributes E(1-t,t). Its quotient tends to zero because its small mass is t^2 and E(1-t,t)=O(t^2 log(1/t)). Thus for any K>2 and lambda>=0, sufficiently rare activations have a control witness regardless of the seed. If the seed is balanced, the added z has B_z=0 exactly: all Fourier sets contain z, since the empty spectrum of f^tensor m vanishes. This gives an additional witness unrelated to a useful seed coordinate.

These facts identify the missing inference, but they alone do not disprove any UNIVERSALLY quantified localized endpoint. A criterion holding on all low-influence functions is stronger than its holding on these activated images.

## 4. Unbalanced activation ledger and actual counterexample to same-K witness transport

To eliminate the balancing-coordinate distraction, use G=f^tensor m WITHOUT z. Let mu=Ef, c=mu^m, and for coordinate i of a seed copy let v_i=fhat({i}) mu^(m-1). Let F=1+A_k(G-1). Its Fourier coefficients are t Ghat(T) times the control-character sign for T nonempty; t(c-1) times that sign for T empty and control set nonempty; and 1-t+tc at the constant set. Hence

    d_data=t d_i(f), d_control=t(1-c),
    I(F)=t[mI(f)+k(1-c)],
    H(F)=t[mH(f)-phi(c^2)] + phi((1-t+tc)^2)
         +(t-t^2)phi((1-c)^2)
         +2k[t(1-c^2)+(t-t^2)(1-c)^2].

The DATA erasure is exactly

    B_data(F)=t B_i(f)-t E(c,v_i)
                +(t-t^2)E(c-1,v_i)+E(1-t+tc,t v_i).             (D)

To derive (D), tensor independence first gives B_i(G)=B_i(f). All pairs whose retained data set is nonempty scale to total weight t. Remove the original empty-retained-data pair, of cost t E(c,v_i), and insert its 2^k-1 nonempty-control pairs and its single constant-control pair. Homogeneity E(su,sv)=s^2 E(u,v) yields (D).

For every CONTROL,

    B_control(F)=t(1-c^2)+(t-2t^2)(1-c)^2
                   +E(1-t+tc,t(c-1)).                         (C)

Its three terms come from nonempty data, nonexceptional empty data, and the exceptional constant pair respectively.

Actual Boolean seed: f=-1 at one point of the three-cube and +1 elsewhere. Its empty Fourier coefficient is 3/4; each nonempty coefficient has magnitude 1/4; every d_i=1/4; and

    B_i(f)=3/8+E(3/4,1/4),
    B_i(f)/d_i=3/2+(5/2)h2(1/10)=2.6724889839... .

Take m=16, k=8, giving dimension 56. Every one of its 48 data coordinates and every one of its eight controls is covered by the exact symmetry of (D)/(C). Rational interval certificates establish

    seed min B_i/d_i > 5/2,
    every activated data B_i/d_i > 5/2,
    every activated control B_i/d_i < 5/2,
    max d_i = 4251920575/1099511627776 < 1/256.

Displays only: seed ratio 2.672488984, data ratio 2.673079974, control ratio 2.059787640. Therefore F satisfies the local selectable inequality with lambda=0,K=5/2, and the seed fails the corresponding inequality at the SAME K. The selected witness must be a control. This refutes the precise POINTWISE implication "if this arbitrary-seed activation admits a K-witness, its seed admits a K-witness." It does not assert that L(tau,0,5/2) holds for every low-influence Boolean function, and so does not refute its global implication.

Moreover, with k=ceil(2log2(m+1)) and m->infinity, this unbalanced construction preserves H/I for every nonconstant seed (|mu|<1). Formula (D) gives B_data/t->B_i(f), while (C) gives B_control/[t(1-c)]->2. To justify the first limit, c,v_i decay exponentially; E(c,v_i) and E(c-1,v_i) tend to zero; and E(1-t+tc,t v_i)/t=O(t v_i^2[1+k+log(1/v_i^2)]) tends to zero, with zero v_i interpreted by continuity. The entropy formula has leading term tmH(f) and overhead O(tk)+o(tm). Thus the same obstruction persists in a ratio-preserving family with arbitrarily weak influences, not just in the fixed 56-variable check.

## 5. Precise missing proof and a conditional same-parameter bridge

If one strengthens the activation endpoint by requiring its witness to lie in an ORIGINAL DATA COPY, then (D), H(F)/I(F)->H(f)/I(f), and d_data=t d_i(f) imply the global selectable inequality with the SAME lambda,K. Select a subsequence with the same seed coordinate i (there are finitely many); irrelevant seed coordinates cannot be chosen. Divide by t d_i and pass to the limit. This establishes the exact sufficient transfer hypothesis.

The first unsupported line in an attempted same-parameter proof from the original localization criterion is: "the localized witness can be chosen among the seed data coordinates, or a control witness can be converted into such a data witness without increasing lambda or K." Section 4 proves that assertion false for ordinary activation. No other witness-preserving embedding is proved here. The global logical implication with identical parameters is OPEN; only finiteness and the explicit FEI constant in section 1 are justified. It would be incorrect to claim a theorem that same constants are impossible for every conceivable reduction.

Proof request to B01/A094: either construct a witness-preserving admission map with all auxiliary influence and erasure paid, or prove a nonlocal control-to-data transfer that survives this one-point-defect tensor witness. This is a mathematical request recorded for supervisor routing, not a question requiring user approval.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

A005/B01 stage 2, noncircular endpoint referee. Full FEI is OPEN. This stage consumes the new B01 minimal-violator dependency rather than repeating the stage-1 activation witness obstruction. All functions are actual finite uniform Boolean signs; H is squared-Fourier Shannon entropy in bits, I total physical influence, B=sum_i H(S_i|S_-i), and D=H-B is dual total correlation. Dimension n always counts RELEVANT coordinates; dummy padding is excluded.

The endpoint under audit is a C-violator f (H(f)>CI(f)) whose one-coordinate children are certified at the SAME finite C>=0. This is the stronger structural endpoint used by the lead and A002's near-erasure bridge. We also distinguish the weaker hypothesis that each pair of children is certified only on average. The assignment is to decide whether an arbitrary seed of ratio R>C can enter this endpoint through a diverging-dimension ratio-preserving embedding, and otherwise identify the precise obstruction.

Result: such an embedding is impossible, even with only average child certification. The obstruction is quantitative: n<=1+(2/ln2)/(H/I-C). This is an endpoint admission obstruction and a review of existing reduction claims, not a new universal entropy payment. The original universally quantified same-parameter low-influence good-coordinate implication remains OPEN; no impossibility for every alternative reduction is asserted.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Complete proof: [DERIVATION.md](DERIVATION.md). The mathematical delta is its signed slack conservation identity, its mechanism-independent admission obstruction, and the exact distinction between individual and average child certification.

For fixed C>=0 let delta=H-CI and let

    sigma_i = average_s [C I(f_i,s)-H(f_i,s)]

be the signed average child slack. Every individual child certified implies sigma_i>=0, but the converse is false. Physical influence recursion and the entropy restriction identity give

    Delta_i H = C Inf_i + delta + sigma_i,
    B_i = C Inf_i + delta + sigma_i - JS_i,
    D + (n-1)delta + sum_i sigma_i = sum_i JS_i.              (1)

This exact identity holds WITHOUT any child certification assumption. In particular negative sigma_i cannot be silently spent as a reserve. Put mu_i=sum_T min(p(T),p(T+i)). The scalar bound is JS_i<=(2/ln2)mu_i and mu_i<=min(Inf_i,1-Inf_i). To reconstruct the scalar interface, set z=2|uv|/(u^2+v^2). The packet divergence in natural units is (u^2+v^2)d(z), where d(z)=[(1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z)]/2. Since d'(z)=atanh(z)<=z/sqrt(1-z^2), integration from zero gives d(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z^2). Consequently the packet is at most (2/ln2)min(u^2,v^2). Summing proves the stated bound.

Assume delta>0 and all sigma_i>=0. D>=0 follows from conditioning reducing entropy and the chain rule. Therefore n>=2 and

    D + sum sigma_i + (n-1)delta
       <= (2/ln2) sum mu_i <= (2/ln2) I,
    0 < H/I-C <= (2/ln2)/(n-1).                             (2)

Thus no sequence of actual Boolean functions F_m can simultaneously satisfy n_m->infinity, H(F_m)/I(F_m)->R>C and certification of all average children at the SAME C. If C varies, necessarily C_m>=R_m-(2/ln2)/(n_m-1), hence liminf C_m>=R. This conclusion applies to every embedding with these output properties, not just activation, tensoring or diffusion. Relevant dimension is essential: adding dummy coordinates does not evade the theorem.

It does not follow that the child-certified endpoint is logically weaker or useless. If ANY C-violator exists, one with the fewest relevant variables exists and all its individual children are certified. Ruling out such witnesses would prove FEI(C) at the SAME C. However an arbitrary larger-gap seed cannot simply be carried into this endpoint while keeping its ratio and sending relevant dimension to infinity. Restriction extraction preserves a positive deficit, not a promised original ratio or fixed gap.

## Independent reconstruction of B01-MINIMAL-VIOLATOR-STRUCTURE@1: HOLDS

With all individual children certified, sigma_i>=0. Equation (1) gives Delta_i>=C d_i+delta>r d_i, because r=H/I=C+delta/I and n>=2 implies d_i<I. Hence B_i>(r-2/ln2)d_i. The entropy bound B_i<=h2(d_i)<=d_i log2(e/d_i) yields d_i<e^3 2^-r. Summation gives D/I<2/ln2 and more precisely D/I<=2/ln2-(n-1)(r-C), as in the original claim. It also yields B/H>1-2/(r ln2). Well ordering of relevant dimension establishes the least-violator hypothesis when any violator exists. This reconstruction uses the exact signed slack identity rather than the lead's numerical calibration. No repair to the original statement was needed; the original statement hash is retained in the local review record.

## Independent reconstruction of A002-NEAR-ERASURE-MAX-CONSTANT-BRIDGE@1: HOLDS

Fix epsilon in (0,1) and suppose H<=C_epsilon I on every Boolean function satisfying B>=(1-epsilon)H. Put C=max(C_epsilon,2/(epsilon ln2)). If FEI(C) fails, choose a least-relevant-dimension violator. Its children are certified; by (2), D<(2/ln2)I. Since H/I>C>=2/(epsilon ln2), D/H<epsilon. Thus the violator lies in the hypothesized near-erasure class and satisfies H<=C_epsilon I<=CI, a contradiction. The reverse direction is subset inclusion. The near-erasure class need not be restriction closed. The existing conditional bridge is accepted at that scope; NO universal bound for its premise is supplied. The new child-certification admission obstruction explains why a direct arbitrary-seed embedding is not the same proof as this minimality argument.

## Exact tensor loss of child certification

For any fixed seed f and C, let delta_0=H(f)-CI(f). Tensor independence gives

    delta(f^tensor m)=m delta_0,
    sigma_(copy,i)(f^tensor m)=sigma_i(f)-(m-1)delta_0.        (3)

If delta_0>0, every average child is eventually a violator. All one-copy decrements and JS payments are unchanged, while the root surplus grows. The final aggregate version is also exact:

    sum sigma(f^tensor m)
       =m sum sigma(f)-mn(m-1)delta_0,

where n is the seed's relevant dimension. The negative term accounts for the loss of child certification; it is not another influence budget.

Actual seed: the three-bit one-point defect has H=4-(9/8)log2 3, I=3/4 and each d_i=1/4. At C=8/3 it has delta_0=2-(9/8)log2 3>0. Its active child is the two-bit point defect, H=2,I=1; its other child is constant. Therefore sigma_i=1/3. Exact rational intervals certify

    1/6 < delta_0 < 1/3.

At m=1 both individual and average certification hold. At m=2, sigma_i=1/3-delta_0>0 for every coordinate, but individual certification fails: the constant branch leaves an untouched violating seed copy. At m=3 and m=4 every sigma_i<0, so average certification also fails. The frozen checker reconstructs actual truth tables and every child spectrum through twelve physical variables.

The rational certificate also proves R_seed-8/3>(2/ln2)/10, with outward lower margin 24062852687/35184372088832>0. Consequently no Boolean function on n>=11 RELEVANT inputs with ratio at least this seed's can have all average children certified at C=8/3. This is an all-dimensional consequence with an exact numerical threshold; it is not an exhaustive eleven-bit census or a disproof of FEI.

First unsupported universal line: prove an absolute-C signed Boolean inequality excluding a positive delta with all sigma_i>=0 while retaining (1)'s complete erasure/JS debt. Equation (1) and the admission obstruction alone do not pay that debt. Likewise, the previously open same-parameter implication for a UNIVERSALLY quantified low-influence selectable criterion is not settled by this audit. A proof request to B01/A002/A055 is now precise: supply the child-certified near-erasure exclusion, or an endpoint transfer that explicitly permits and pays the loss of the original ratio gap. Asking for another diverging-dimension ratio-preserving child-certified embedding contradicts (2).

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

SOURCE ~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\agents\A006\runs\20261011T210855Z-fleet05-A006-posterior-forest-optimization\REPORT.md
# A006 posterior forest optimization

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All entropies and logarithms below are in bits except the rank discrepancies F, which use natural logarithms. The universal target G-J-COST remains open. This run solves the finite posterior optimization for one explicit unbounded-read representation, and supplies certified computable bounds for other finite collections.

For every integer m>=1, take uniform signs x0,...,xm and let

    f_m = 2*1[x0=1]*1[there is an i>=1 with xi=1]-1.

Represent f_m as the union of the m gates h_i=(x0 AND xi). These are distinct actual functions on supports R_i={0,i}, not repeated descriptions. Order each support as (0,i), set M_i=1, pi_i=1/4, lambda_i=1/m, and gamma=6/5. This is one fixed generator for each fixed function, independent of the sampled Fourier set. The incidence sum is 1 at coordinate 0 and 1/m at every private coordinate.

Write a=2^(-6/5), D=(1+1/(4m))^m-1, Lambda=m log(1+1/(4m)), and Z=1-2^(-Lambda). Thus Lambda+log Z=log D, without dropping either normalization term.

The optimized residual over **all distinct actual partition-forest runs** in this representation obeys

    R_m <= (43/10) I(f_m).

An explicitly paid prior over admissible scaled complete references gives residual plus scale-selection charge <=(53/10) I(f_m). Independently, the exact conditioned run-cost optimum has spectral expectation <=(23/2) I(f_m), or <=(25/2) I(f_m) including that same prior charge. All are upper bounds for a particular representation, not assertions about the optimal universal representation.

This family is already a read-once Boolean formula and a weighted threshold (weight m on x0, weight 1 on each xi). Its FEI status was therefore already covered by baseline results. The contribution is the **complete posterior cost and deterministic-witness obstruction for its given DNF with read number m**, not a new Boolean FEI class or a novelty claim.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

The execution protocol was read in full: fei-execution-protocol/2, revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2. The board snapshot is 2026-10-11 21:04 UTC. B02/STREAM_NOTES.md was absent when checked; no direction was inferred from that absence. A006 had no previous run output. No B02 prior run exists in the provided reports tree.

The reports README, CURRENT_STATUS, claim/obstruction registries, packet index and stream next actions were inspected. The research plan's Endpoint J, equations forest/residual, and A006 acceptance contract fix the target. The actual complete proof source is source_material/reductions/paper/forests.tex, Section `sec:forests`, definitions `eq:forestpacking`, `eq:forestconditioning`, `eq:forestcost`, and theorem `thm:posteriorforest`. Its generator uses probability lambda_child for each child gate, with remaining mass failure. This differs from the uniform-neighbor generator in the public threshold paper; the difference is retained here.

The supplement CAMPAIGN, CLAIMS, COUNTEREXAMPLES, NATIVE_MAPPING, and SEED_LEMMAS were consulted; SEED_LEMMAS Section 2 supplies the normalized mixture interface, with every selected complete law and scale charge retained. The baseline current-threshold-paper.tex Sections 5.2, 5.6 and Proposition `prop:joint-witness`, and its exact forest checker were read. Context threshold_paper_38pp.txt Section 5.6 is the corresponding public interface; the percolation paper Section 2 gives a different hierarchical set codec and is not used as a proof premise. Earlier paper versions were inspected for context, not imported as dependencies. Source scopes and SHA256 pins are saved in out/SOURCES.json. No external theorem beyond these source-complete coding interfaces and elementary finite entropy identities is required.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Optimization and computable truncation certificates

For a finite set Omega of actual runs and any finite cost b(r), put S=sum_{r in Omega} 2^(-b(r)). If S>0, then

    inf_p [sum_r p(r)b(r)-H(p)] = -log S,
    p_*(r)=2^(-b(r))/S.

Indeed, the expression equals -log S + KL(p || p_*). Infinite-cost/zero-weight runs must have p=0 at any finite optimum; if all weights vanish the value is +infinity. Unsuccessful or infinite queue paths are not in Omega. The actual partition-forest set is finite. For a countable successful-run collection and b=-log q, increasing finite collections converges to -log sum q whenever this sum is positive; finite-support posteriors suffice to establish the infimum even if the full conditional entropy is infinite.

This identity is baseline bookkeeping. Its computational use is as follows. For a deduplicated enumerated subcollection E, S_E gives an upper bound -log S_E on the full infimum. If every omitted run has cost >=b0 and their number is <=N, then

    -log(S_E+N*2^(-b0)) <= full infimum <= -log S_E.

More generally an upper bound U on the omitted weight replaces N*2^(-b0). Interval bounds on S_E and U yield rigorous logarithm intervals with their directions preserved. In the forest residual b=B; for the exact complete run cost b=-log q, S is actual conditioned output mass of the selected runs. These are different optimizers: p_B need not equal the true posterior p_q.

If a description map d->r has multiplicities n_r, summing descriptions would wrongly insert n_r in S. Either canonicalize to the actual queue key (initial root set, chronological visited-gate/subset/ordered-child trace), or use H(description)-H(description|run)=H(run). The correction follows directly from the entropy chain rule since run is a function of description. Child-label draws in different occurrence slots at the same attachment variable really are different random events; permutations of the written edge list which leave those draws unchanged are not.

For rational incidence weights and gamma=6/5, each 2^(-B) is algebraic: rank and congestion factors are rational quantities raised to rational powers. Rational bisection for fifth roots, products and finite sums gives explicit rational intervals for S; logarithms can then be bounded by a convergent rational series. No assertion of efficient enumeration in arbitrary dimension is made.

### 2. Exact Boolean law and influence

Let b=prod_{i=1}^m (1-xi)/2. Then

    f_m=x0-(1+x0)b.

Consequently P(empty)=4^(-m), P({0})=(1-2^(-m))^2, and P(T)=P({0} union T)=4^(-m) for every nonempty private T. Parseval gives v=1-4^(-m). Direct pivotal probabilities are I_0=1-2^(-m) and I_i=2^(-m) for private i. Hence

    I(f_m)=1+(m-1)2^(-m) >=1.

### 3. Exhaustion of actual partition forests

For A={0}, exactly m runs exist: choose root r, subset {0}, and zero children. Each has B=log m+2. They differ in the activated gate, so posterior H=log m is justified.

For A=T nonempty private, every coordinate is forced to its own gate, no incidence edges exist, all t=|T| gates are roots, and there is exactly one run. Its cost is

    B_private=t(log m+16/5).

Here F_i^i=ln 2 since the common predecessor is absent.

For A={0} union T, assignment of coordinate 0 determines a unique source gate. All other used gates own only their private variable, and have no outgoing edge. Thus the forest is forced to be a star. There are two possibilities:

* Inside root: r in T owns {0,r}, other gates own their private singleton. There are t choices of r and (t-1)! distinct ordered child-label sequences, for t! runs. Every such run has B_in=t log m+2+(6/5)(t-1).
* Outside root: r outside T owns {0}, with t private leaves. There are m-t root choices and t! ordered child-label sequences, for (m-t)t! runs. Each has B_out=(t+1)log m+2+(6/5)t.

In both cases every child attaches at coordinate 0. The chronological child draws are part of the run; selected subsets are disjoint and every used gate is visited once. This exhausts all assignments, root choices, parents, attachments and permitted child orders, with no duplicated descriptions.

The complete residual partition weights are therefore

    S_B({0})=1/4,
    S_B(T)=m^(-t)*(a/4)^t,
    S_B({0} union T)= t! * a^(t-1)/(4*m^t) * [1+(1-t/m)*a].

Together with p_B(r) proportional to 2^(-B(r)), these determine the exact finite optimizer for every spectral set. In the last display, individual runs inside each root type have equal weight, but the two root types have different weights.

### 4. Dimension-independent residual payment

Using the preceding exact optima,

    R_m = v log D + 2(1-2^(-m))^2
      + 4^(-m) sum_{t=1}^m binom(m,t) *
        [2t log m +(22/5)t +4/5 -log(t!)
         -log(1+(1-t/m)*a)].

The root normalizer is well controlled in both directions:

    1/4 <= D < e^(1/4)-1 < 1/3.

The lower bound is the linear binomial term; (1+u)^m<=e^(mu) proves the middle bound. Since exp(x)<1/(1-x) for 0<x<1 by termwise comparison of its series with the geometric series, exp(1/4)<4/3. In particular log D<0, but the exact formula retains it before this bound.

Drop the two nonpositive posterior terms only for the upper estimate. The identities sum binom(m,t)t=m2^(m-1) and log m<=m give the rare-set contribution at most

    2^(-m) [m log m +(11/5)m +(4/5)(1-2^(-m))]
    <= 2^(-m) [m^2 +(11/5)m +4/5] <=23/10.

For the last inequality the values at m=1,2 are 2 and 23/10. If P(m)=m^2+(11/5)m+4/5, then 2P(m)-P(m+1)=m^2+(1/5)m-12/5>=0 for m>=2; induction proves the bound for all integers. The dominant term is <=2. Thus R_m<=43/10<= (43/10)I(f_m).

Posterior credit is indispensable for this representation. For point-mass posteriors, every singleton-common witness costs log m+2; every other B is nonnegative. Since log D>=-2,

    R_deterministic >= (1-2^(-m))^2*(log m+2)-2v.

The right side diverges while I(f_m)->1. This is an all-m obstruction to deterministic forest residual payment in this fixed DNF, **not** an obstruction to other representations or to FEI.

### 5. Exact complete run costs, beyond the residual bound

The actual subset sampler has weights theta_1(0)=1, theta_1(i)=a and theta_2=1. With nonempty roots conditioned, a prescribed root set of size k has mass (1/(4m))^k/D. Multiplying every prescribed draw gives total masses of all partition runs:

    W({0})=1/[16(1+a)D],
    W(T)=D^(-1) * [a/(16m(1+a))]^t,
    W_in({0} union T)= t!/[24 D t^2(t+1)m^t]
                          * [a/(4(1+a))]^(t-1),
    W_out({0} union T)= (m-t)t!/[8 D m^(t+1)(1+a)(t+1)(t+2)]
                           * [a/(4(1+a))]^t.

For inside roots the root subset-size probability is 1/6, the child-count probability is 1/[t(t+1)], and the attachment multiset probability is 1/t. For outside roots those factors are 1/[2(1+a)], 1/[(t+1)(t+2)], and 1. Every leaf costs a/[4(1+a)], and each prescribed child-gate label has probability 1/m. Summing all distinct sequences gives the factorials. Thus W_common=W_in+W_out. The exact optimized whole-cost expectation C_m is

    C_m=(1-2^(-m))^2 * log(1/W({0}))
        +4^(-m) sum_{t=1}^m binom(m,t)
           [log(1/W(T))+log(1/(W_in+W_out))].

This optimizes over all partition forests, not over successful runs with repeat visits or overlapping subsets. The latter may improve the reference output mass further and are not needed here.

For an elementary uniform bound, a<1 and 1/a<4 imply (1+a)/a<5. Since D<1/3, the dominant cost is <log(32/3)<4. Private-set cost is at most t log(80m)<=t(m+7). For common sets use only W_in, drop -log(t!), and bound log 24<5, log 20<5 and log(m+1)<=m. This gives

    log(1/W_common) <= 5+3m+t(m+5).

Adding the two rare costs and using the binomial identities bounds their full spectral expectation by

    2^(-m)(m^2+9m+5) <=15/2.

The initial value is 15/2 at m=1, and for Q(m)=m^2+9m+5 one has 2Q(m)-Q(m+1)=m^2+7m-5>=0 for m>=1. Hence C_m<=23/2<= (23/2)I(f_m). No K_ref component, terminal cost or hidden label was omitted: this calculation uses actual complete probabilities.

### 6. A paid normalized prior

For k>=0, build the entire conditioned forest reference using lambda_i^(k)=2^(-k)/m, and assign the fixed prior

    w_k=1/[(k+1)(k+2)].

The weights telescope to 1. Every component satisfies fractional incidence; each has its own Lambda_k, Z_k, and exact complete queue costs. Define Q_mix=sum_k w_k Q_{*,k}. This is a subprobability by nonnegative summation. No scale is chosen for free after observing A. Choosing k=0 for the displayed upper bound costs log(1/w_0)=1 bit under the normalized-prior lemma. Thus the whole-cost expectation is at most C_m+v<=(25/2)I(f_m); the K_ref form is at most K_ref I+R_m+v<=(K_ref+53/10)I. This uses the seed mixture interface on complete references and pays the selector, instead of adding a free adaptive scale to B.

The original two-sided dyadic prior from SEED_LEMMAS is unnecessary here; this one-sided prior is explicitly normalized and every component is admissible. Its scale is relative to the globally fixed base incidence vector, not an output-dependent incidence choice.

### 7. Boundary of the result

No line in the stated sunflower theorem remains unsupported by the derivation above. Independent acceptance is pending. The sunflower calculation does not establish it. Nor does it settle shared-data collision classes with arbitrary Boolean cores.

# A006 stage 2: dictionary review and majority prime-implicant forests

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Following the B02 lead, this stage independently reconstructs A008 v1, then addresses PI-J on every odd-dimensional majority function. Units are bits for H, posterior entropy and B; natural logarithms for rank F; I=E|A| on the uniform cube. Full FEI and the all-Boolean PI-J target remain open.

For every odd n>=1, let k=(n+1)/2 and f=Maj_n. Use **all prime positive implicants**, the conjunctions on the k-subsets R of [n]. Let N=binom(n,k), d=binom(n-1,k-1), lambda_R=1/d, pi_R=2^-k, and one ordered cover per gate, the restriction of the same physical order 1,...,n. These globally fixed weights satisfy every incidence constraint with equality. No function/set-dependent scale selector is used. Let

    D=(1+2^-k/d)^N-1=2^Lambda Z,
    G(A)=sum_{j in A} ln[j/(|A intersect [j-1]|+1)].

I prove the all-dimensional upper bound for the **optimized actual partition-forest residual**:

    R_PI(Maj_n) <= [49/5+(6/5)log2(e)] I(Maj_n)
                < (58/5) I(Maj_n) <= (58/5)sqrt(n).

The proof retains posterior entropy and root conditioning in one fixed reference. It bounds the true infimum above by explicit realizable posterior laws; it does not identify that infimum from finite experiments. Exact all-partition sums for n=1,3 and rigorous constructed upper bounds for n=5,7 are saved separately.

Majority was already an FEI class. This stage proves a cost theorem for its specified prime-implicant reference, not a new FEI class or full FEI. A008's distinct dictionary reference is independently accepted at its original scope, without repair.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent A008 acceptance

AUDIT_A008.md reconstructs normalized finite dictionary sampling, its output pushforward, finite prefix decoding, canonical root/attachment recovery, all sunflower output masses, the <25I/4 and <33I/4 bounds, and the latent-name lower bound. No author code was imported and no repair was contributed. Verdict: HOLDS at the exact original statement. In particular output-code credit is not a reduction in bits needed to reveal the latent record, and it is not a bound on the original B residual.

### 2. Why the majority gates are exactly the primes

A positive subcube certificate for majority must force at least k positive coordinates: if fewer are fixed positive, set every remaining free coordinate negative and the function is false. Fixed negative coordinates cannot aid a minimal positive certificate. A conjunction fixing exactly k coordinates positive is sufficient, and removal of any one literal makes it insufficient. Thus the prime positive implicants are precisely all k-subsets. Every coordinate lies in d such gates, hence lambda=1/d is admissible. All supports are ordered by the same physical order; the sampler is globally fixed.

Since majority is balanced, v=1 and the binary empty/nonempty term is zero. Put r=N lambda pi=(n/k)2^-k. Because n/k<=2 and k>=1, r<=1. We first retain the exact root block Lambda+log Z=log D. Then

    D <= exp(r)-1 <= r exp(r),
    log D+k <= log(n/k)+r log(e) <3.

The last bound uses log(n/k)<=1 and log2(e)<2, which follows from ln2>1/2. Failed and infinite runs supply no output mass and are never inserted into posterior entropy.

### 3. Small Fourier sets: posterior over all containing clauses

Fix any nonempty A with s=|A|<=k. Every gate R containing A yields a distinct actual run: R is the sole root, it selects B_R=A, chooses zero children, and terminates. There are C_s=binom(n-s,k-s) such runs. Their root names differ, so their uniform posterior has entropy log C_s. These are not alternative descriptions of a single event.

For each such run, M=1, kappa=s and the congestion term is zero. If j is the t-th selected coordinate, all A predecessors of j lie in R, and its gate rank is at most its physical rank j. Hence F_R^j(A)<=ln(j/t). Therefore

    log D+E B-H(posterior)
       <= log D+k+log d-log C_s+(6/5)/ln2 * G(A).

The full label/root block, rather than an independent per-coordinate label, is present before bounding it. The containment ratio satisfies

    log[N/C_s]=log[binom(n,s)/binom(k,s)] <=2s.

To prove this for every s<=k, write the logarithm as sum_{j=0}^{s-1} log[(n-j)/(k-j)]. Its increments are nondecreasing, so the average of its first s increments is at most the average of all k. The total at k is log binom(n,k)<=n<=2k. Also d/N=k/n<=1. Thus log d-log C_s<=2s and

    small-set cost <=3+2s+(6/5)/ln2 * G(A)
                   <=5s+(6/5)/ln2 * G(A).

There is no paid model switch here: the posterior selects different runs of the same fixed generator.

### 4. Large Fourier sets: two actual gates suffice

For s>k, split the selected coordinates alternately in their physical order into U and V, with |U|=ceil(s/2), |V|=floor(s/2), and choose j=min U. Choose a gate R containing U and a gate T containing V union {j}, with a fixed global lexicographic tie rule. They exist because |U|<=k and |V|+1<=k for s<=n=2k-1. They must differ: otherwise one k-set would contain A, impossible when s>k.

Assign U to R and V to T; root R, child T attached at j. The ownership graph contains R->T. If it also contains T->R, both are in one source SCC; otherwise R is the unique source. In either case root R is permitted. Thus this is a genuine once-per-gate finite partition run with one root. Prescribed size, child-count, multiset and label probabilities are positive; selected subsets are disjoint and decode exactly A. A point mass on this run has posterior entropy zero, stated explicitly rather than implicitly dropped.

Every gate has congestion kappa/s_l<=s/floor(s/2)<=3, since this regime has s>=3. Its total congestion cost is <(12/5)s because log3<2. The gate-name cost is 2 log d<=2(n-1)<4s. At the t-th selected coordinate, the assigned block has at least (t-1)/2 previous coordinates in the integer sense: previous block members plus one equal ceil(t/2), which is >=t/2. Full A predecessors within the gate may only increase this denominator. Its gate rank is <=j, so

    F_owner^j(A) <=ln(j/t)+ln2.

The rank increment costs at most (6/5)s. Root rarity and global root normalization together cost <3, as in Section 2. Thus

    large-set cost <=3+(38/5)s+(6/5)/ln2 * G(A)
                   <=(43/5)s+(6/5)/ln2 * G(A),

using 3<=s. The small-set bound is also <=this expression. All gate-label, congestion, rarity and rank charges remain in this derivation.

### 5. Spectral expectation and all-dimensional bridge

The construction proves the following pointwise-to-expectation statement for the fixed complete middle-layer table, even for an arbitrary spectral law supported on nonempty sets:

    constructed residual <=(43/5) E|A|+(6/5)/ln2 * E G(A)

when that law has v=1. With v<1 the same statement holds with the unconditioned nonempty expectation, since the bound is pointwise. This statement is about the coding table and does not assume that the input Boolean function is its OR; majority supplies exactly that OR and its prime interpretation.

For majority, every earlier coordinate is as desirable as every later coordinate. The j-pivotal derivative is the indicator of the middle Hamming slice on n-1 coordinates; its supports on predecessor fibers are antichains. The baseline signed relative-noise/rank proof therefore gives

    E_{nu_j} ln[j/(|T intersect [j-1]|+1)] <=1+ln2.

The exact derivative Fourier identity is P_f(T union {j})=I_j nu_j(T). Multiply by I_j and sum j to obtain

    E_{P_f} G(A) <=(1+ln2) I(f).

No estimate from the fractional-overlap theorem or unknown FEI conclusion is used here. Substitution gives the displayed residual constant 49/5+(6/5)log2(e). The elementary atanh series gives ln2=2 sum_{l>=0}(1/3)^(2l+1)/(2l+1)>2/3, so this constant is strictly less than 58/5.

Finally I(Maj_n)=n*2^{-(n-1)}*binom(n-1,(n-1)/2). The exact antichain-density recurrence bounds the factor by 1/sqrt(n), hence I<=sqrt(n). The claimed O(sqrt n) bound follows for all odd n, with no extrapolation from finite cases.

The constructed law is merely an upper certificate for the optimized residual over every actual partition forest. No claim that the large-set deterministic witness, or the small-set uniform posterior, is optimal is made. No prior term is missing: every witness used belongs to one fixed complete reference, and no sampled-output choice of a reference table or scale occurs.

### 6. Exact finite optimization and logarithmic certificates

For n=1,3 the checker exhausts all assignments to containing gates, computes source SCCs, chooses each permitted root set, checks parent reachability, enumerates attachments and child-label orders, and deduplicates by actual chronological queue trace. In an assigned forest let

    z=product_l (s_l/kappa_l)^(s_l)
         *product_{j in A}[(|A intersect U_j^owner|+1)/rank_owner(j)],
    c=lambda^(number used)*pi^(number roots).

Then 2^-B=c*z^(6/5), exactly. The full sum of these weights is stored as a finite algebraic expression; its optimum is -log of that sum. Exact trace identity, not abstract witness multiplicity, determines the count. Fifth roots are enclosed by integer comparisons on a 48-bit dyadic grid. Logarithms use the 18-term atanh expansion after dyadic range reduction, with remainder

    2*z^37/[37(1-z^2)]

for z=(y-1)/(y+1), 1<=y<=2. Interval division uses all endpoint quotients, including negative numerators. Summing signed bounds gives rigorous intervals for the residual. The files distinguish full optimization at n=1,3 from constructive upper bounds at n=5,7.

The exact n=1 residual is zero by root cancellation. At n=3 the rigorous full optimum interval is [1.302334214,1.302334215]. At n=5,7 the constructed residuals lie in [2.888679153,2.888679154] and [4.252815747,4.252815748], respectively. These latter intervals enclose costs of the selected construction, not the full infimum. The finite checker optimizes within each small-set one-root cohort by weights proportional to 2^-B; this can only improve on the uniform posterior used in the analytic proof. For large sets it uses the one prescribed two-gate run.

There is no first unsupported line in the stated all-odd-majority cost bound; it awaits independent review. The first unsupported universal line is an absolute expected bound on the analogous rank-weighted prime-cover/forest mass for **every** Boolean function. The global G(A) bound used here is a regular-function input and is false in general: A094's XOR-of-conjunction stress gives diverging single-order rank/I. It must not be promoted to a universal premise.

# A006 stage 3: actual forest mass from determinant moments

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Packet A006, stream B02, C020 / PI-J. For every fixed finite nonconstant gate table, positive feasible fractional lambda, covered nonempty set A and eligible ownership assignment tau, I derive the exact surrogate partition mass over DISTINCT ACTUAL source-SCC partition-forest queue events. Entropy and B costs are in bits; gamma=6/5 and the baseline F statistics are in natural logarithms. The formula is b_tau times a factorial-moment evaluation of a rooted Laplacian determinant polynomial. Canonical-order determinants give a computable lower mass, and weighted determinants and e! give computable upper masses, where e is the number of forest edges. Summing over assignments bounds the complete optimized E B-H posterior cost. Parent, root and attachment choices alone do not count every actual run.

I independently accept the WHOLE A008 unequal read-once DNF forest-payment v1 at its original scope, without repair. The new determinant interface is a combinatorial cost calculation for arbitrary references, not a new universal O(I) claim or a new known-class FEI calibration. It is analytically proved and exactly checked, pending independent review. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

FOREST_MOMENTS.md gives the complete proof and exact boundary. For a fixed assignment, used gates V form the incidence graph u->v iff A_u intersects R_v. Let c be its number of source SCCs, e=|V|-c, and b_tau=2^(-B_nonroot). Introduce x_(u,j) for each owned coordinate. Edge weight w_uv=sum_(j in A_u intersects R_v)x_(u,j); use the incoming Laplacian L_vv=sum_u w_uv, L_vu=-w_uv. Define

    T_tau(x)=sum_(one root in each source SCC) product_root pi_r * det L_(nonroots,nonroots)(x).

Multilinearity of the rows selects a parent for every nonroot. Cycle choices vanish by linear dependence; acyclic choices have determinant one after parent-first ordering. Expanding each edge weight selects its attachment coordinate. Thus T is the exact attached-forest polynomial with nonnegative coefficients. This proof fixes orientation and root constraints without relying on an external matrix-tree assertion.

An attached forest with h_(u,j) children at one repeated attachment has product h! distinct sequences of child-label draws. Root order and order between different attachment coordinates stay fixed. The resulting BFS event uniquely recovers the assignment, parents, attachment multiset and chronological label sequence, so there is no extra credit for alternative descriptions. Different assignments are disjoint actual run sets. Let M map each monomial product x^h to product h!, equivalently expectation over independent rate-one exponential variables. Then

    S(A)=sum_tau b_tau M(T_tau),
    inf_eta [E B-H(eta)]=-log2 S(A).

The second identity is the already-established finite variational baseline, not a new claim. The new content computes its correct actual-run mass. This is a SURROGATE B mass, not exact queue probabilities, whose subset/count normalizers remain covered by the source K_ref I overhead.

Put T_can=T(1) and D_(u,j)=number of possible child gates at group (u,j). With rational a_D=(D+1)/2 for D>=1 (a_0=1), define T_up=T(a_D). Integer AM-GM proves h!<=a_D^h for h<=D; the multinomial coefficient proves product h!<=e!. Therefore

    T_can <= M(T) <= min(e! T_can,T_up).

Sharper rational enclosures a_D>=(D!)^(1/D) also work. For rational pi all determinant bounds are exactly rational. b_tau is generally algebraic at gamma=6/5 and is kept separate; it can be enclosed outward for numeric entropy bounds. Summation of b_tau times the lower and upper masses gives reversed -log bounds on the full optimized posterior cost. A fixed-assignment canonical posterior already attains the upper cost -log2(b_tau T_can). Bounds coincide when all D<=1. The factorial gap is sharp on actual sunflower prime tables, with e! distinct child sequences at a forced star.

SCC block triangularity gives a product of grounded non-source SCC determinants and source SCC sums of rarity-weighted principal cofactors. This computes T_can/T_up in polynomially many exact determinant operations at a fixed assignment, avoiding root-set enumeration. It does not remove the assignment sum or make polynomial factorial-moment evaluation generally cheap. Expectation cannot be factored across SCCs: a three-gate actual prime-table star has T=pi_1*x_(1,0)^2, moment 2pi_1, while multiplying SCC-factor expectations gives pi_1. The new exact boundary avoids that false shortcut.

REVIEW_A008.md reconstructs all of A008's residual <47I/10, actual nonempty cost <=[26/5+2log2(3)+2log2(e)]I, and entropy split <67I/5. Physical spectra, root odds, unique partition events and the all-width conditional-binomial payment are reconstructed independently; no repair is supplied.

The first unsupported UNIVERSAL line remains: for one globally fixed feasible prime-table reference chosen from every arbitrary Boolean f,

    v log2[product_l(1+lambda_l*pi_l)-1]
       - E_P log2[sum_tau b_tau M(T_tau)] <= K I(f)

with dimension-independent K. The exact mass calculation does not prove this expectation bound, and log2(e!) cannot be uniformly bounded by a constant times set size. No universal FEI conclusion is inferred from the finite certificates or the arbitrary-reference identity.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

STAR_COMPLETION.md proves the exact arbitrary-graph construction. A maximal matching M in G[A] leaves an independent unmatched set U. Each unmatched u chooses any neighbor nu(u); it lies either at a matched coordinate or outside A. Matching gates own pairs. Unmatched gates own singleton u, are children of the gate owning nu(u) when that coordinate is matched, and are isolated roots otherwise. No two singleton gates can coincide, since that would require an edge within U. The entire ownership-incidence graph is exactly a disjoint union of outgoing stars and isolated roots, so every specified event obeys the original source-SCC root restriction.

For matched e={v,w}, let h_v,h_w be child counts at the two attachment coordinates and c_e=h_v+h_w; let b be the number of outside-A neighbor choices. The exact mass for M is

    Q_M(A)=D^-1 product_(e in M)(lambda_e/24)
              *sum_nu [product_(u in U)(lambda_(u,nu(u))/8) *4^-b
                         *product_(e={v,w} in M) h_v!h_w!/((c_e+1)^2(c_e+2))],
    D=product_all_edges(1+lambda_e/4)-1.

The matched root pays pi*t_pair=1/24; a singleton child pays t_singleton*Pr(c=0)=1/8; an external singleton root additionally pays pi=1/4. A parent star pays its count law and the uniform two-coordinate multiset factor, and h_v!h_w! counts only genuine chronological child-label sequences. Initial roots and different attachment coordinates stay in fixed order. Both endpoint orders make original theta identically one, so t_singleton=1/4 and t_pair=1/6 exactly.

The actual queue's pair-owned subsets recover M, singleton subset/gate IDs recover nu, and appended-child draws recover parent, attachment and chronological choices. Thus different matchings, choices and slot permutations are disjoint actual events. Summing all maximal M gives Q_MS(A)<=Q_part(A)<=Q_*(A). It is strictly positive for every covered A. At conditional posterior eta(r)=q(r)/Q_MS(A), E[-log q]-H(eta)=-log Q_MS(A); this is output aggregation rather than credit for duplicate descriptions or a physically free record.

Using (c+1)^2(c+2)<=2*6^c and discarding h!>=1 gives the explicit lower bound

    Q_M(A)>=D^-1*48^(-(|M|+|U|))*product_(e in M)lambda_e*product_(u in U)r_u,
    r_u=sum_(v neighbor u)lambda_uv.

Summing that bound over maximal matchings gives a computable positive L_MS(A), so complete cost is at most -log2 L_MS(A). It specializes on independent sets to the already-known exact mass D^-1 product_(u in A)(r_u/32). That specialization is a consistency check, not a repeated independent-sector theorem. Enumerating maximal matchings can be exponential; no polynomial-time claim is made.

REVIEW_A008.md independently reconstructs the WHOLE formal A008 complete-mass v1, unchanged statement hash, including actual event bijection, root odds, all operator factors, beta/exponential/simplex moments, coefficient bounds, final ambiguity and universal boundary. Its formal statement HOLDS without repair. The auxiliary sentence 'each t_u=1/2 (uniform singleton choice)' in the source's star example is ERROR: on width two, both orders give t_singleton=1/4, and one order gives theta/[2(1+2^-gamma)]. This sentence is not used in the formal mass identity or stripped-star coefficient. I record formal acceptance with that scoped source error, not unqualified source acceptance. A009's matching proof correctly uses 1/4 and is unaffected. Any consumer that actually substituted 1/2 for t must recompute its probabilities.

The exact first unsupported all-graph line is an influence-paid expectation of the full mass, for one globally fixed feasible vector:

    Var(f_G)log2 D - sum_(A!=empty)P_f(A)log2 [sum_M 48^(-(|M|+|U|))
                                     product_(e in M)lambda_e product_(u in U)r_u]
       <=K I(f_G)

with absolute K. Positivity and finite <5I certificates do not establish this line. A sharper posterior should preserve the exact factorial factor above instead of discarding it. Nothing in this stage replaces raw indicator-sum error by conditioned local norms, the substitution A014 has refuted. Higher-width/arbitrary Boolean prime tables still lack this maximal-matching star decomposition and universal payment.

# A007 root normalization

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All log denotes log_2; all entropies are in bits. For every finite nonconstant data indicator B=OR_l b_l with rho_l=E b_l>0, every r>=1, and every fixed positive incidence vector lambda with sum_l lambda_l<=1, consider F=2 A_r B-1, where A_r is a conjunction on r fresh fair coordinates. Write p=2^(-r), b=E B, g=2B-1, I_data=p I(g), I_core=2rp b, v=Var(F). Use the conditioned queue of the reductions manuscript on the actual gates h_l=2 A_r b_l-1. Their root parameters are pi_l=p rho_l (no truncation ambiguity). Ordered covers can be chosen arbitrarily for the main component theorem.

There exist actual partition-forest laws with root count K satisfying

    T_root := v(Lambda+log Z) + E sum_{roots l} log(1/pi_l)
       = v log s + v delta + r(E K-v) + E sum_{roots l} log(1/rho_l),
    s=sum_l lambda_l rho_l,
    0<=delta=log[(product_l(1+p lambda_l rho_l)-1)/(p s)]<=p s log e,
    0<=r(E K-v)<=I_data/2,
    v delta<= (log e) I_core.

Consequently T_root<=E sum_roots log(1/rho_l)+(log e) I(F). The favorable v log s remains explicit in the exact identity before this estimate. This settles the common-core rarity/normalization component uniformly in dimension, read count, r, and base gate probabilities; it does not pay base rarity, labels, ranks, congestion, or complete witness cost.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json was read in full, revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2. The board snapshot read at the start and before validation was 2026-10-11 21:55 UTC.
